Proof of Convergence

Proof of Theorem
2
.

Similar to the proof of Theorem
1
, the proof starts with the base case at
t=0
and proceeds to analyze the training dynamics in a deductive manner, providing additional details in deriving the corresponding convergence and sample complexity bounds.

(
S1
) Warm-up (Base case):
Training dynamics at the first iteration
t=0
.

Recall that we set
\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(0)}=\bm{0}
. Then, we have

\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(0)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle=0,\quad\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(0)},\bm{o}_{-}\rangle=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(0)},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle=0\quad\forall j.

(
S1.1
) Training dynamics of
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}
at the first iteration
t=0
.

From Lemma
C.1
, identify
p_{1}=0
,
q_{1}=0
,
p_{2}=0
and
q_{2}=0
. Let
\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}
and
\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}
be the distance between two
\bm{o}_{+}
and
\bm{o}_{-}
features respectively in the positive sample. Similarly, in a negative sample, let the distance between the two

\bm{o}_{+}
tokens as
\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}
, and the distance between the two

\bm{o}_{-}
tokens as
\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}
.
Then, for any lucky neuron
i\in\mathcal{W}(0)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{c^{\prime 2}}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(0)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right]+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

(125)

\displaystyle\text{and }\quad\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(0)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)\quad\text{for }j\neq 1.

(126)

Recall that we set the number of samples in a batch
N=\mathrm{poly}(d)
.

Suppose the initialization is

\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}(0)=\delta_{1}\bm{o}_{+}+\delta_{2}\bm{o}_{-}+\cdots+\delta_{d}\bm{o}_{d},\quad\delta_{j}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\xi^{2})\quad j=1,2,\cdots,d.

(127)

Then, after one gradient descent step, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{1}+\frac{\eta c^{\prime 2}}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top}}^{(1)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\delta_{1}+\frac{\eta}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right]+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

(128)

\text{and}\quad\left\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top}}^{(1)},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)\quad\text{for }j\neq 1.

(129)

By applying Lemma
C.3
, for any lucky neuron
i\in\mathcal{U}(0)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\delta_{2}+\frac{\eta c^{\prime 2}}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}-2}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top}}^{(1)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\delta_{2}+\frac{\eta}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}}\right]+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

(130)

\text{and}\quad\left\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top}}^{(1)},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)\quad\text{for }j\neq 2.

(131)

For any unlucky neuron
i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}\setminus\mathcal{U}(0)
, Lemma
C.4
gives

\left\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top}}^{(1)},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)\quad\text{for }\forall j.

(132)

(
S1.2
) Training dynamics of
\bm{w}_{\Delta}
at the first iteration
t=0
.

Now consider the gradient update for
\bm{w}_{\Delta}
. Define:

a=\delta_{1}+\frac{\eta c^{\prime 2}}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

b=\delta_{1}+\frac{\eta}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right]+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

Applying Lemma
C.5
with
p_{1}=0
,
q_{1}=0
,
r_{1}^{*}=a
,
s_{1}^{*}=b
, and
\rho^{+}_{0}=|\mathcal{W}(0)|
, we get

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(0)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq\frac{a}{4\sqrt{m}}\cdot\rho^{+}_{0}-\frac{b\sqrt{m}}{8}-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

(133)

We can relax this lower bound and obtain

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(0)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq\frac{c^{\prime}}{4}\left[\frac{2a}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\rho^{+}_{0}-\sqrt{m}b\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)=:\alpha

(134)

Recall that
\delta_{1}=\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}
.
Since
a-b=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)
that is sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=\frac{c^{\prime}}{4}\left[\frac{2a}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\frac{m}{2}-\sqrt{m}b\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{c^{\prime}}{4}\left[\sqrt{m}a-\sqrt{m}a\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

\displaystyle=0-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)\approx 0

(135)

From Lemma
C.6
, we also obtain

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(0)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\frac{-a}{8\sqrt{m}}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}\cdot\rho^{+}_{0}+\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}\cdot\rho^{-}_{0}\right]

(136)

where we apply the lemma with the values

p_{1}=0,\quad q_{1}=0,\quad q_{2}=0,\quad\text{and}\quad r_{1}^{*}=a.

We can relax this upper bound and obtain

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(0)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\frac{-ac^{\prime}}{4\sqrt{m}}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}\cdot\rho^{+}_{0}+\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}\cdot\rho^{-}_{0}\right]=:\gamma

(137)

Taking
\rho^{+}_{0}=\rho^{-}_{0}=\frac{m}{2}+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)
, we can simplify and write

\begin{split}\gamma&=\frac{-ac^{\prime}}{4\sqrt{m}}\cdot\frac{m}{2}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}+\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}\right]\\
&=-\sqrt{m}a\cdot\frac{c^{\prime}}{8}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}+\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}\right]\\
&=\frac{-\eta c^{\prime 3}}{16L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right]\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}+\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right).\end{split}

(138)

(
S2
) Induction Step: Training dynamics at a general iteration
t
.

Let
\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle=\alpha^{*}\geq\alpha\cdot t
,
\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{-}\rangle=\beta^{*}\geq\beta\cdot t
, and
\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle=\gamma^{*}\leq\gamma\cdot t
, where

\beta=\frac{c^{\prime}}{4}\left[\frac{2a^{\prime}}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\rho^{-}_{0}-\sqrt{m}b^{\prime}\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)>0

(139)

a^{\prime}=\delta_{2}+\frac{\eta c^{\prime 2}}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}-2}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

b^{\prime}=\delta_{2}+\frac{\eta}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}}\right]+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

Following the same approach as in (
135
), we can simplify and obtain

\beta=-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right).

(140)

For any lucky neuron
i\in\mathcal{W}(t)
at the
(t+1)
-th iteration, we have

\begin{split}&\frac{c^{\prime 2}}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma(\alpha^{*})\left[\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)\\
&\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\\
&\quad\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma(\alpha^{*})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(\alpha^{*})\right)^{2}\left[\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)\end{split}

(141)

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(1)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)\quad\text{for }j\neq 1

(142)

Note that,
\sigma(\alpha^{*})>\frac{1}{2}
and
\sigma(\gamma^{*})<\frac{1}{2}
.

Thus, we obtain the following bound after the second gradient descent step.

\begin{split}&\left\langle(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(2)})^{\top},\,\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\\
\geq&\delta_{1}+\frac{\eta c^{\prime 2}}{\sqrt{m}L}\Bigg[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-1}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-1}\\
&+\sigma(\alpha^{*})\left(\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right)\Bigg]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right).\end{split}

(143)

and

\begin{split}&\left\langle(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(2)})^{\top},\,\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\\
\leq&\delta_{1}+\frac{\eta}{2\sqrt{m}L}\Bigg[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}}\\
&+2\sigma(\alpha^{*})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(\alpha^{*})\right)^{2}\left(\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right)\Bigg]+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right).\end{split}

(144)

For the convenience of presentation, we use
u
to denote the lower bound in (
143
), and
v
to denote the upper bound in (
144
).

Similarly, applying Lemma
C.3
to any lucky neuron
i\in\mathcal{U}(1)
at iteration
2
, we get

\begin{split}\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(1)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\geq&\frac{c^{\prime 2}}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma(\beta^{*})\left[\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}-2}-\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\right]\\
&\qquad-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)=:u,\end{split}

(145)

\begin{split}\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(1)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\leq&\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma(\beta^{*})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(\beta^{*})\right)^{2}\left[\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}-2}-\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\right]\\
&\qquad+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)=:v,\end{split}

(146)

and

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(1)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)\quad\text{for }j\neq 1

(147)

Applying Lemma
C.5
with
p_{1}=\alpha^{*}
,
q_{1}=\alpha^{*}
,
r_{1}^{*}=u
, and
s_{1}^{*}=v
, we obtain

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(1)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\sigma(\alpha^{*})c^{\prime}}{2}\left[\frac{2u}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\rho_{1}^{+}-\sqrt{m}v\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)=:\chi

(148)

Since
\rho_{1}^{+}=\frac{m}{2}
and
u-v=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)
that is sufficiently small, we have
\chi\approx 0
.

By applying Lemma
C.6
with

p_{1}=\alpha^{*}(=\beta^{*}),\quad q_{1}=\alpha^{*}(=\beta^{*}),\quad q_{2}=\gamma^{*},\text{and}\quad r_{1}^{*}=u,\text{we have}

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq-\frac{c^{\prime}u}{2\sqrt{m}}\sigma(\alpha^{*})\left[\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}\rho_{t}^{+}\;+\;\left(1-\sigma(\gamma^{*})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}\rho_{t}^{-}\right]=:\iota

(149)

Note that here we assumed the distribution of
\Delta L^{+}
is identical to
\Delta L^{-}
to have
\alpha^{*}=\beta^{*}
.

(
S3
) Induction conclusion: Training dynamics when the algorithm ends.

We proceed by induction on
t
: the base case
t=0
is established in (S1), and the induction step for general
t
is shown in (S2).
For, any lucky neuron
i\in\mathcal{W}(T)
, we obtain

\left\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top}}^{(T)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq aT

(150)

\left\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top}}^{(T)},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)\quad\text{for }j\neq 1

(151)

For any lucky neuron
i\in\mathcal{U}(T)
, we obtain

\left\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top}}^{(T)},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\geq aT

(152)

\left\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top}}^{(T)},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)\quad\text{for }j\neq 2

(153)

Also, we obtain

\left\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(T)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq\alpha T,

(154)

\left\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(T)},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\geq\beta T,

(155)

\text{and}\quad\left\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(T)},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\gamma T.

(156)

(
S4
) Derivation for the generalization bound.

We will demonstrate that once the weights have converged at iteration
T
, the model accurately captures the underlying data distribution, which leads to zero generalization error, as shown in (
180
).

Consider
z^{(n)}=+1
as an example.
The sequence
\bm{X}^{(n)}=\begin{bmatrix}\bm{x}_{1}^{(n)}&\bm{x}_{2}^{(n)}&\cdots&\bm{x}_{L}^{(n)}\end{bmatrix}
contains two
\mathbf{o}_{+}
at
L_{1}^{+}
and
L_{2}^{+}
and two
\mathbf{o}_{-}
at
L_{1}^{-}
and
L_{2}^{-}
.

\displaystyle F(\bm{X}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\sum_{i=1}^{m}v_{i}\,\phi\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}^{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}^{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}(0)}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{U}(0)}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}^{-}\setminus\mathcal{U}(0)}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)

(157)

The Mamba output
\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}
is defined as

\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}=\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{l}\left(1-\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{j}^{(n)})\right)\right)\cdot\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})\cdot(\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)\top}\bm{x}_{l}^{(n)})\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}.

(158)

We now derive a lower bound for

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}(0)}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}).

To that end, consider the aggregated projection

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}(0)}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}(0)}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle\cdot\langle\bm{y}_{l},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle.

(159)

For any
i\in\mathcal{W}(0)
, we know that

\langle\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\geq aT.

(160)

Hence, let’s obtain a lower bound for
\langle\bm{y}_{l},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle

We only need to consider the cases where
\bm{x}_{s}=\bm{o}_{+}
for some
s
in the range
1\leq s\leq l
. In particular, we will focus on the following instances:

s=L_{1}^{+}\text{ and }l\in\{L_{1}^{+},L_{2}^{+}\},\qquad s=L_{2}^{+}\text{ and }l=L_{2}^{+}.

After
T
iterations, we know

\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\geq\alpha T,\quad\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{-}\rangle\geq\beta T,\quad\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle\leq\gamma T\quad\text{for }j\neq 1,2.

(161)

Therefore,

\left\langle\bm{y}_{L_{1}^{+}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=\sigma\left(\left\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\right)\geq\sigma(\alpha T).

(162)

We have,

\left\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq W_{1}T,\qquad\left\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\leq W_{2}T,

where

\displaystyle W_{1}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right),

(163)

\displaystyle W_{2}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right).

(164)

Then we obtain the following:

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{y}_{L_{2}^{+}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\sigma(\alpha T)+\left(1-\sigma(W_{1}T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(W_{2}T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle)\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}\cdot\sigma(\alpha T)

\displaystyle=\sigma(\alpha T)\left[1+\left(1-\sigma(W_{1}T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(W_{2}T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle)\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}\right].

(165)

We now lower bound the objective

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}(0)}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}).

We begin with

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}(0)}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l})\geq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}(0)}\left[\phi(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{L_{1}^{+}})+\phi(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{L_{2}^{+}})\right].

Note that

\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{L_{1}^{+}}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\langle\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\left\langle\bm{y}_{L_{1}^{+}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle,

and
\bm{y}_{L_{1}^{+}}
has only
\bm{o}_{+}
component.

Therefore,

\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{L_{1}^{+}}=\left\langle\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\left\langle\bm{y}_{L_{1}^{+}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq aT\cdot\sigma(\alpha T)>0.

Similarly, we can write

\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{L_{2}^{+}}\geq aT\cdot\sigma(\alpha T)\left[1+\left(1-\sigma(W_{1}T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(W_{2}T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle)\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}\right]>0.

Applying
\phi(z)=z
for positive
z
, we obtain

\phi(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{L_{1}^{+}})\geq aT\cdot\sigma(\alpha T),

\phi(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{L_{2}^{+}})\geq aT\cdot\sigma(\alpha T)\left[1+\left(1-\sigma(W_{1}T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(W_{2}T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle)\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}\right].

Hence,

\begin{split}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}(0)}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l})&\geq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{W}(0)}aT\cdot\sigma(\alpha T)\cdot\\
&\quad\left[2+\left(1-\sigma(W_{1}T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(W_{2}T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle)\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}\right].\end{split}

(166)

Next, we derive an upper bound for

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{U}(0)}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right).

For any
i\in\mathcal{U}(0)
, we know that

0<\langle\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top},\bm{o}_{-}\rangle\leq bT.

(167)

We now derive an upper bound for
\left\langle\bm{y}_{l},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle
.
We need to focus on the following instances:

s=L_{1}^{-}\text{ and }l\in\{L_{1}^{-},L_{2}^{-}\},\qquad s=L_{2}^{-}\text{ and }l=L_{2}^{-}.

\left\langle\bm{y}_{L_{1}^{-}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle=\sigma\left(\left\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\right)\leq\sigma(W_{2}T).

(168)

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{y}_{L_{2}^{-}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sigma(W_{2}T)+\left(1-\sigma(\alpha T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\beta T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle)\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\cdot\sigma(W_{2}T)

\displaystyle=\sigma(W_{2}T)\left[1+\left(1-\sigma(\alpha T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\beta T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle)\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\right].

(169)

Hence,

\begin{split}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{U}(0)}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l})&\leq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{U}(0)}bT\cdot\sigma(W_{2}T)\cdot\\
&\quad\left[2+\left(1-\sigma(\alpha T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\beta T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle)\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\right].\end{split}

In addition, we have

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}^{-}\setminus\mathcal{U}(0)}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right).

(170)

By (
157
), we can write

\begin{split}F(\bm{X}^{(n)})&\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\Biggl\{\frac{m}{2}\cdot aT\cdot\sigma(\alpha T)\left[2+(1-\sigma(W_{1}T))(1-\sigma(W_{2}T))(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}\right]\Biggr.\\
&\qquad\Biggl.-\frac{m}{2}\cdot bT\cdot\sigma(W_{2}T)\left[2+\left(1-\sigma(\alpha T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\beta T)\right)\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle)\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)\Biggr\},\end{split}

(171)

with

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta c^{\prime 2}}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right),

(172)

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad b

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right]+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right).

(173)

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=\frac{c^{\prime}}{4}\left[\frac{2a}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\rho^{+}_{0}-\sqrt{m}b\right]-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

(174)

Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle F(\bm{X}^{(n)})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\Biggl\{\frac{m}{2}\cdot aT\cdot\sigma(\alpha T)(1-\sigma(W_{1}T))(1-\sigma(W_{2}T))

\displaystyle\left[(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\right]\Biggr\}-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}\right)

(175)

If we can show
\left[(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\right]>0
, then we can prove
F(\bm{X}^{(n)})\geq C
for some positive constant
C
.

First define a random variable
\psi_{1}=\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle
. Then, we have from the definition of our locality-structured data type

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(1-\sigma(\psi_{1}))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-(1-\sigma(\psi_{1}))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\right]=k^{\prime}>0\\
\end{split}

(176)

for some positive constant
k^{\prime}
.

The random variable
\psi_{2}=(1-\sigma(\psi_{1}))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-(1-\sigma(\psi_{1}))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}
is bounded above by 1.

Applying Hoeffding’s bound, for any
q>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\psi_{2}-\mathbb{E}\psi_{2}\right|\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{q\log N}{N}}\right)\leq N^{-q}.

(177)

From this we can conclude that,

\psi_{2}=\left[(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\right]\geq k^{\prime}-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{q\log N}{N}}\right),

(178)

with probability at most
N^{-q}
.

Hence, for sufficiently large N, we have from (
176
)

\left[(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{j}\rangle))^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}-2}\right]>0

(179)

Therefore,

\displaystyle F(\bm{X}^{(n)})

\displaystyle\geq C,\text{ where $C$ is some positive constant.}

(180)

Similarly, for a negative sample, one can show by symmetry that the model output satisfies
F(\bm{X}^{(n)})\leq 1
.

(
S4.1
) Derivation for the convergence rate.

Let’s find the number of iterations
T
required such that
F(\bm{X}^{(n)})\geq 1
, since the label is
+1
. We require

\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\frac{m}{2}\cdot aT\cdot\sigma(\alpha T)\geq 1+\epsilon.

(181)

Substituting the value of
a=\frac{\eta}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right]
and
\sigma(\alpha T)\approx\frac{1}{2}
since
\alpha\approx 0
, the condition becomes

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{m}aT}{4L}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{m}}{4L}\cdot\frac{\eta}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right]T

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta T}{8L^{2}}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right]\geq 1+\epsilon.

(182)

Solving for
T
, we obtain

T\geq\frac{8L^{2}(1+\epsilon)}{\eta\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right]}\geq\frac{8L^{2}}{\eta\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right]}.

(183)

By combining (
181
) with the expression for the model output
F(\bm{X}^{(n)})
in (
175
), we obtain

\displaystyle F(\bm{X}^{(n)})

\displaystyle\geq(1+\epsilon)

(184)

Hence, the model output satisfies
F(\bm{X}^{(n)})\geq 1
.

(
S4.2
) Derivation for the sample complexity.

Now we derive a sample-complexity bound that guarantees zero generalization error.

Assuming enough samples, we can write for sufficiently small
\lambda\ll 1

\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)\leq\lambda\cdot\frac{\eta}{2\sqrt{m}L}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right].

(185)

From this we can derive a lower bound on the required sample size,

\begin{split}N&\geq\Omega\left(\lambda^{-2}\cdot\frac{4L^{2}d}{\eta^{2}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right]^{2}}\right)\\
&\geq\Omega\left(\frac{L^{2}d}{\eta^{2}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}-\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right]^{2}}\right),\end{split}

(186)

which will be (
19
) in Theorem
2
.

∎

Appendix D
Proof of Lemmas in Appendix
B

Please refer to the supplementary material or a preliminary work at
(
Shandirasegaran et al. 2025
)
for this section. We defer all proofs to the supplementary material, as the high-level ideas underlying the lemmas overlap with those presented in Appendix
C
for locality data. However, the case of locality-structured data presents additional challenges. Appendix
E
provides the complete proofs for the locality-structured data, which contain the main technical ideas.

D.1
Proof of Lemma
B.1

Proof.

We know that the gradient of the loss function for the
n^{\text{th}}
sample is

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{\ell}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\mathcal{\ell}}{\partial F(\bm{X}^{(n)})}\cdot\frac{\partial F(\bm{X}^{(n)})}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}.

(187)

If we consider the gradient for the population loss,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}}

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right]

(188)

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right].

(189)

We are given that

p_{1}\leq\left\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq q_{1},\quad\text{and}\quad p_{2}\leq\left\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\leq q_{2}.

(190)

The Mamba output can be written as

\displaystyle\bm{y}_{l}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{l}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{x}_{j})\right)\right)\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s})\cdot(\bm{x}_{s}^{\top}\bm{x}_{l})\bm{x}_{s}

(191)

We have to consider TWO cases.

Case I:
for
l=1,2,\ldots,\alpha_{r}L

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}\bm{y}_{l},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left(\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)^{l-s+1}\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)

(192)

Case II:
Others

For the other token positions,
\bm{x}_{l}\neq\bm{o}_{+}
. Since we assume orthogonality among the features,
\bm{y}_{l}=0
.

From our initialization, for the lucky neuron
i\in\mathcal{W}(0)
,
v_{i}=+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}
. For
i\in\mathcal{W}(0)
, and
z^{(n)}=+1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{r}L}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{r}L-(k-1)\right)\right]\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(193)

Similarly for
z=-1
, we can have the feature
\bm{o}_{+}
for
l=\alpha_{r}L+1,\ldots,(\alpha_{r}+\alpha_{c})L
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}\bm{y}_{l},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=\alpha_{r}L+1}^{l}\left(\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)^{l-s+1}\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)

(194)

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{c}L}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{c}L-(k-1)\right)\right]\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(195)

Therfore, combining (
193
) and (
195
),

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

(196)

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{r}L}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{r}L-(k-1)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt\left.-\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{c}L}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{c}L-(k-1)\right)\right]\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(197)

Follow the similar proof of Lemma
C.1
, from (
275
) – (
302
), the deviation between the population and empirical gradients along
\bm{o}_{+}
satisfies

\begin{split}\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)&\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\\
&\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).\end{split}

(198)

By pairing (
197
) with the given the conditions on
\bm{w}_{\Delta}
in (
190
), we can write

\begin{split}&\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\\
\geq&\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{r}L}\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{r}L-(k-1)\right)-\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{c}L}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{c}L-(k-1)\right)\right]\\
&\quad-\mathcal{O}(\tau)\end{split}

(199)

and

\begin{split}&\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\\
\leq&\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{r}L}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{r}L-(k-1)\right)-\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{c}L}\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{c}L-(k-1)\right)\right]\\
&\quad+\mathcal{O}(\tau)\end{split}

(200)

Therefore, we can obtain the lower bound and the upper bound of
\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle
as

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma(p_{1})}{\sqrt{m}L}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{r}L}\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{r}L-(k-1)\right)-\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{c}L}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{c}L-(k-1)\right)\right]-\mathcal{O}(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

(201)

\displaystyle\text{and }\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma(q_{1})}{\sqrt{m}L}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{r}L}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{r}L-(k-1)\right)-\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{c}L}\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{c}L-(k-1)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(202)

This concludes the proof of (
28
) and (
29
) in Lemma
B.1
.

To obtain
\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle
, we have to consider
\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right]
.

If
{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}\bm{o}_{-}>0
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\!\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{r}L}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{r}L-(k-1)\right)\right]\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(203)

If
{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}\bm{o}_{-}\leq 0
,

\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(204)

From (
189
), We know that

\begin{split}\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle&=\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\\
&\quad-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle.\end{split}

(205)

Hence, combining both cases, we conclude

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{r}L}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{r}L-(k-1)\right)\right]-\mathcal{O}(\tau)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(206)

From (
300
), similar to (
198
), we can write

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle-\mathcal{O}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(207)

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{r}L}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{r}L-(k-1)\right)\right]-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(208)

This concludes the proof of (
30
) and (
31
) in Lemma
B.1
.

Now consider
\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\text{ for }j\neq 1,2
.

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle:=\left\langle I_{1},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle I_{2},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle.

(209)

Because
\bm{o}_{j}\text{ for }j\neq 1,2
is identical in both
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
,
\left\langle I_{1},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle I_{2},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)
. Hence,
\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)
.
From (
300
), similar to (
198
), we can write

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(210)

Therefore,

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau)\text{ for }j\neq 1,2.

(211)

This concludes the proof of (
32
) in Lemma
B.1
.

∎

D.2
Proof of Lemma
B.2

Proof.

By definition, for any unlucky neuron
i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}\setminus\mathcal{W}(0)
, we have

\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{o}_{+}\leq 0.

(212)

We first consider the alignment with
\bm{o}_{+}
. That is,

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle.

(213)

The gradient is given in (
189
).
We only need to consider the cases where
\left\langle\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle>0
.
However, since
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{o}_{+}\leq 0
, we have

\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)=0.

(214)

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(215)

We know by (
198
),

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(216)

Hence,

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(217)

We now analyze the alignment with
\bm{o}_{-}
. To obtain the bound on

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle
,
we consider the expectation

\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right].

If
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}\bm{o}_{-}>0
, the inner product satisfies

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{r}L}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{r}L-(k-1)\right)\right]\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)

(218)

If
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}\bm{o}_{-}\leq 0
, then

\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(219)

From (
264
), We know that

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle.

(220)

Hence, combining both cases, we conclude

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{r}L}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{r}L-(k-1)\right)\right]-\mathcal{O}(\tau)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(221)

From (
300
), similar to (
198
), we can write

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(222)

Hence,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha_{r}L}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)^{k-1}\left(\alpha_{r}L-(k-1)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(223)

Now consider
\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\text{ for }j\neq 1,2
.

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle:=\left\langle I_{1},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle I_{2},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle.

(224)

Because
\bm{o}_{j}\text{ for }j\neq 1,2
is identical in both
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
,
\left\langle I_{1},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle I_{2},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)
. Hence,
\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)
.
From (
300
), similar to (
198
), we can write

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(225)

Therefore,

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau)\text{ for }j\neq 1,2.

(226)

∎

D.3
Proof of Lemma
B.3

By symmetry, the proof is analogous to that of Lemma
B.1
; Please see Appendix
D.1
.

D.4
Proof of Lemma
B.4

By symmetry, the proof is analogous to that of Lemma
B.2
; Please see Appendix
D.2
.

D.5
Proof of Lemma
B.5

Proof.

The gradient of the loss with respect to
\bm{w}_{\Delta}
for the
n^{\text{th}}
sample is given by

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{\ell}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left(\bm{W}_{B}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}\right)^{\top}\left(\bm{W}_{C}^{\top}\bm{x}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\sigma\left(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\prod_{r=s+1}^{l}\left(1-\sigma\left(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{r}^{(n)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\left(1-\sigma\left(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}\right)\right)\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}-\sum_{j=s+1}^{l}\left(1-\sigma\left(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{j}^{(n)}\right)\right)\bm{x}_{j}^{(n)}\right]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{l}\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)}.

(227)

We define the gradient summand
\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)}
as

\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)}=\beta_{s,s}\cdot\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}-\sum_{j=s+1}^{l}\beta_{s,j}\bm{x}_{j}^{(n)},

(228)

where the coefficients
\beta_{s,s}
and
\beta_{s,j}
are given by

\displaystyle\beta_{s,s}

\displaystyle=(\bm{W}_{B}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})^{\top}(\bm{W}_{C}^{\top}\bm{x}_{l}^{(n)})(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\prod_{r=s+1}^{l}\left(1-\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{r}^{(n)})\right)\right](1-\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})).

(229)

and

\displaystyle\beta_{s,j}

\displaystyle=(\bm{W}_{B}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})^{\top}(\bm{W}_{C}^{\top}\bm{x}_{l}^{(n)})(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\prod_{r=s+1}^{l}\left(1-\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{r}^{(n)})\right)\right](1-\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{j}^{(n)})).

(230)

If we consider the gradient of the empirical loss,

\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}}=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{l}\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)}.

(231)

We are given that

r_{1}^{*}\leq\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t+1)}}^{\top},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\leq s_{1}^{*}.

(232)

From our initialization, for all
i\in\mathcal{K}^{+}
, we have
v_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}
. This gives

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{\ell}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle.

(233)

Averaging over the training samples, the inner product of the empirical gradient becomes

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n:z^{(n)}=+1}\frac{1}{L}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right)\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n:z^{(n)}=-1}\frac{-1}{L}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right)\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\right].

(234)

First, we focus on the contribution from samples with
z^{(n)}=+1
and neurons

i\in\mathcal{K}^{+}
, for which we aim to establish a lower bound. To analyze the inner terms, we distinguish two cases:
(i) the tokens contain the feature
\bm{o}_{+}
;
(ii) they do not, in which case the inner product vanishes due to orthogonality.

Case I:

l=1,2,\ldots,\alpha_{r}L
.

For fixed
l
and
s\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
, we write

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=\begin{cases}\beta^{(l)}_{s,s},&s=l,\\[4.0pt]
0,&s=l-1,\\[4.0pt]
-\beta^{(l)}_{s,s+1},&1\leq s\leq l-2,\end{cases}

(235)

where the coefficients depend on
l
(hence the superscript).

In particular, the diagonal term can be written as

\beta^{(l)}_{s,s}=\left\langle\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\,\sigma\left(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\right)\,\left(1-\sigma\left(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\right)\right),

(236)

which is independent of token position within the block (since it occurs at the last position
s=l
).

The off-diagonal terms
\beta^{(l)}_{s,s+1}
exhibit additional multiplicative decay due to the recursive gating structure. Specifically,

\beta^{(l)}_{s,s+1}=\left\langle\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{\top},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\cdot\sigma\left(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma\left(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\right)\right)^{t},

(237)

where
t=l-s+1
denotes the effective gating depth from position
s
to
l
.

Given the conditions in (
232
), we can write

\begin{split}\beta^{(l)}_{s,s}&\geq\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)r_{1}^{*}-\mathcal{O}(\tau)),\\
\text{and}\quad\beta^{(l)}_{s,s+1}&\geq\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)^{(l-s+1)}\cdot r_{1}^{*}-\mathcal{O}(\tau)).\end{split}

(238)

Here, we assumed
p_{1}\leq\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\leq q_{1}
in addition to the conditions in (
232
) and also included the effect of the noise as well.

More specifically,

\begin{array}[]{l}l=1:\quad\langle\bm{I}_{1,1}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle=\beta^{(1)}_{1,1}.\\[4.0pt]
l=2:\quad\langle\bm{I}_{2,2}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle=\beta^{(2)}_{2,2},\;\langle\bm{I}_{2,1}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle=0.\\[4.0pt]
\vdots\\[4.0pt]
l=\alpha_{r}L:\quad\langle\bm{I}_{l,l}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle=\beta^{(l)}_{l,l},\;\langle\bm{I}_{l,l-1}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle=0,\;\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle=-\beta^{(l)}_{s,s+1}\quad(1\leq s\leq l-2).\end{array}

(239)

Case II:
The tokens do not contain the feature
\bm{o}_{+}

In such cases,
\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=0
due to orthogonality among the features.
Therefore, the total contribution over all tokens is bounded as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}\cdot L}\cdot\left[\alpha_{r}L\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{t}\right]r_{1}^{*}-\mathcal{O}(\tau))

(240)

Let
\rho_{t}^{+}=|\mathcal{W}(t)|
be the number of contributing neurons. Then the total contribution from the active neurons is lower bounded as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\rho_{t}^{+}\cdot\left[\alpha_{r}L\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{t}\right]r_{1}^{*}-\mathcal{O}(\tau)).

(241)

Next, we consider the case
z^{(n)}=-1
for
i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}
, where we aim to establish an upper bound.
For negative samples, the tokens contain
\alpha_{c}L
instances of the feature
\bm{o}_{+}
, specifically for

l=\alpha_{r}L+1,\alpha_{r}L+2,\ldots,(\alpha_{r}+\alpha_{c})L
.
As in the above case, we distinguish two scenarios:

Case I:

The token contains the feature
\bm{o}_{+}
, with the same contribution structure as above (see Eqs. (
235
)–(
238
)).

Case II:

The token does not contain
\bm{o}_{+}
, so
\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=0
by orthogonality.

The total contribution from the negative sample is thus upper bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\left[\alpha_{c}L\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)^{t}\right]s_{1}^{*}+\mathcal{O}(\tau)).

(242)

The maximum number of such contributing neurons is
\frac{m}{2}
. Therefore, the total contribution is bounded above by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{m}}{2L}\cdot\left[\alpha_{c}L\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)^{t}\right]s_{1}^{*}+\mathcal{O}(\tau)).

(243)

Rearranging terms, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{m}s_{1}^{*}}{2L}\cdot\left[\alpha_{c}L\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)^{t}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\tau)).

(244)

Thirdly, let us consider the contribution for
z^{(n)}=+1
from
i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}
.
From our initialization, for
i\in\mathcal{K}^{-}
,
v_{i}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}
. For
z^{(n)}=+1
, we seek an upper bound on the contribution from such neurons.

Let
z^{(n)}=+1
. To maximize the term
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}
in (
229
), we consider token positions that contain
\bm{o}_{-}
features, since
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}
has a large component in the
\bm{o}_{-}
direction. Then
\bm{x}_{l}=\bm{o}_{-}\Rightarrow\bm{y}_{l}
contains the
\bm{o}_{-}
feature.

However, in this case,
\bm{x}_{s}=\bm{o}_{-}=\bm{x}_{l}
, and due to orthogonality,

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=0.

(245)

Therefore, we only need to consider the time steps
l=1,2,\ldots,\alpha_{r}L
, where the
\bm{o}_{+}
features appear.
Recall the gradient expression:

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle.

(246)

We have,

\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{o}_{+}\leq\delta_{1}+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)=:c,

(247)

and combining with (
232
), similar to (
240
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\left[\alpha_{r}L\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{t}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\tau)).

(248)

Since there are at most
\frac{m}{2}
such contributing neurons in
\mathcal{K}_{-}
, the full contribution is bounded as:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\frac{m}{2}\cdot\left[\alpha_{r}L\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{t}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\tau)).

(249)

Therefore, the overall contribution from these neurons satisfies:

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\sqrt{m}c}{2L}\cdot\left[\alpha_{r}L\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{t}\right]-\mathcal{O}(\tau)).

(250)

Finally, we consider
z^{(n)}=-1
for
i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}
.
For
z^{(n)}=-1
, we want a lower bound since
v_{i}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}
.

We could consider
l=L^{+}\Rightarrow\bm{x}_{l}=\bm{o}_{+}
, and write

\left\langle\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq\delta_{1}-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(251)

However, the minimum number of such contributing neurons is not tractable. Thus, if we consider the worst case where
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}
for
i\in\mathcal{K}^{-}
does not learn the
\bm{o}_{+}
feature, the obvious lower bound is zero:

\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq 0.

(252)

We now combine the bounds for the four terms identified in (
234
), corresponding to the contributions from:
(i)
\mathcal{K}_{+}
with
z^{(n)}=+1
as shown in (
241
),
(ii)
\mathcal{K}_{+}
with
z^{(n)}=-1
as shown in (
244
),
(iii)
\mathcal{K}_{-}
with
z^{(n)}=+1
as shown in (
250
), and
(iv)
\mathcal{K}_{-}
with
z^{(n)}=-1
as shown in (
252
). We assume the batch is balanced, so the number of positive and negative samples is equal, with each class contributing
\frac{N}{2}
samples. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\Biggl[\frac{r_{1}^{*}}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\rho_{t}^{+}\cdot\left(\alpha_{r}L\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\sqrt{m}c}{2L}\cdot\left(\alpha_{r}L\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\sqrt{m}s_{1}^{*}}{2L}\cdot\left(\alpha_{c}L\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)^{t}\right)+0\Biggr]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathcal{O}(\tau))

(253)

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{1}^{*}}{2\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\rho_{t}^{+}\cdot\left(\alpha_{r}L\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\sqrt{m}s_{1}^{*}}{4L}\cdot\left(\alpha_{c}L\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)^{t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau)),

(254)

where we have used the fact
-\frac{\sqrt{m}c}{2L}\cdot\left(\alpha_{r}L\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{t}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)
since
c=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)
.

Taking
\left(\alpha_{r}L\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)^{t}\right)=\Theta(\alpha_{r}L)
and
\left(\alpha_{c}L\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)-\sum_{k=3}^{\alpha_{r}L}\sum_{t=4}^{k+1}\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)^{t}\right)=\Theta(\alpha_{c}L)
, we can write more compactly as

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq\frac{r_{1}^{*}}{2\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\rho_{t}^{+}\cdot\Theta(\alpha_{r}L)-\frac{\sqrt{m}s_{1}^{*}}{4L}\cdot\Theta(\alpha_{c}L)-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau))

(255)

∎

D.6
Proof of Lemma
B.6

Proof.

The gradient is given in (
227
).

Let’s consider the alignment with
\bm{o}_{k}\text{ for }k\neq 1,2
.

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{\ell}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle=\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle

(256)

From our initialization, for all
i\in\mathcal{K}^{+}
, we have
v_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}
.

We first consider the case
z^{(n)}=+1
for
i\in\mathcal{K}^{+}
.
Since
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}
, for
i\in\mathcal{K}^{+}
has a large
\bm{o}_{+}
component, we have to consider the token features with
\bm{o}_{+}
.
For
z^{(n)}=+1
, only when
l=1,2,\ldots,\alpha_{r}L
we have
\bm{x}_{l}=\bm{x}_{s}=\bm{o}_{+}
.
Therefore,
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{x}_{s}
is significant. Hence, we have for
l\neq s

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=s+1}^{l}\beta_{s,j}\langle\bm{x}_{j}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\rangle

\displaystyle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)).

(257)

Hence, we obtain

\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}(\tau)).

(258)

Similarly for
i\in\mathcal{K}^{-}
, for
z^{(n)}=-1
also we have

\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}(\tau)).

(259)

Putting it together, We know

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n:z^{(n)}=+1}\frac{1}{L}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right)\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n:z^{(n)}=-1}\frac{-1}{L}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right)\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\right].

(260)

Therefore, we have

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}(\tau))+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(261)

Note that the second and third terms in (
260
) yield the minority contribution of
\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)
.

∎

Appendix E
Proof of Lemmas in Appendix
C

E.1
Proof of Lemma
C.1

Proof.

We know that the gradient of the loss function for the
n^{\text{th}}
sample is

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{\ell}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\mathcal{\ell}}{\partial F(\bm{X}^{(n)})}\cdot\frac{\partial F(\bm{X}^{(n)})}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}.

(262)

If we consider the gradient for the population loss,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}}

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right]

(263)

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right].

(264)

We are given that

p_{1}\leq\left\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq q_{1},\quad p_{2}\leq\left\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\leq q_{2},\quad\text{and}\quad p_{3}\leq\left\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq q_{3}\quad\text{for }j\neq 1,2.

(265)

The Mamba output can be written as

\displaystyle\bm{y}_{l}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{l}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{x}_{j})\right)\right)\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s})\cdot(\bm{x}_{s}^{\top}\bm{x}_{l})\bm{x}_{s}

(266)

We have to consider FOUR cases.

Case I:

l=s=L_{1}^{+}

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{s}

\displaystyle=\bm{x}_{l}=\bm{o}_{+}

(267)

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}\bm{y}_{L_{1}^{+}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\geq\sigma(p_{1})\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)=\frac{1}{1+e^{-p_{1}}}\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(268)

Case II:

l=s=L_{2}^{+}

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}\bm{y}_{L_{2}^{+},L_{2}^{+}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(269)

Case III:

l=L_{2}^{+}
,
s=L_{1}^{+}

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}\bm{y}_{L_{2}^{+},L_{1}^{+}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{j})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(270)

Combining (
269
) and (
270
), we obtain

\begin{split}\left\langle\mathbb{E}\bm{y}_{L_{2}^{+}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle&=\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\\
&\quad+\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)\\
&\quad\cdot\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{j})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).\end{split}

(271)

Case IV:
Others

For the other token positions,
\bm{x}_{l}\neq\bm{o}_{+}
. Since we assume orthogonality among the features,
\bm{y}_{l}=0
.

From our initialization, for the lucky neuron
i\in\mathcal{W}(0)
,
v_{i}=+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}
. For
i\in\mathcal{W}(0)
, and
z^{(n)}=+1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\!\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\left[2+\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\cdot\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{j})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}\right]\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(272)

Similarly for
z=-1
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\!\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\left[2+\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\cdot\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{j})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right]\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(273)

Therfore, combining (
272
) and (
273
),

\begin{split}\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle&=\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\!\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\\
&\quad-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\!\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\\
&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\cdot\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)\cdot\\
&\quad\left[\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{j})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{j})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right]\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).\end{split}

(274)

We aim to bound the deviation between the gradient of the population loss and that of the empirical loss. Specifically,
\left\|\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\mathbb{E}\bm{\gamma}_{n}\right\|_{2}
, where

\bm{\gamma}_{n}=\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}.

(275)

Consider a fixed vector
\bm{\alpha}
with
\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{2}=1
.
We will show that
\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}
is a sub-Gaussian random variable.

\left|\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}\right|\leq\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{2}\cdot\|\bm{\gamma}_{n}\|_{2}=\|\bm{\gamma}_{n}\|_{2}.

(276)

By the problem setup, we know that

|v_{i}|=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}},\quad|z^{(n)}|=1,\quad\left|\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\right|\leq 1.

(277)

Recall the Mamba output,

\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}(t)=\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left(\prod_{j=s+1}^{l}\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{x}_{j})\right)\right)\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s})\cdot(\bm{x}_{s}^{\top}\bm{x}_{l})\bm{x}_{s}.

(278)

Since
\|\bm{x}_{s}\|_{2}=1
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left|a^{l-s+1}\cdot\left(\bm{x}_{s}^{\top}\bm{x}_{l}\right)\right|\cdot\|\bm{x}_{s}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=1}^{l}\frac{a}{1-a}\cdot 1\cdot 1=a^{\prime}\quad\text{(where $a^{\prime}$ denotes a constant).}

(279)

Therefore, the norm of
\bm{\gamma}_{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\gamma}_{n}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}|v_{i}|\cdot\left|\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\right|\cdot\left\|\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{L}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left\|\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{L}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{L}a^{\prime}=\frac{a^{\prime}}{\sqrt{m}}.

(280)

Hence,

\left|\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}\right|\leq\frac{a^{\prime}}{\sqrt{m}}\quad\text{(bounded).}

(281)

This implies that
\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}
is sub-Gaussian with variance proxy

\sigma^{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{m}\right).

(282)

Now consider the independent sub-Gaussian variables
\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{1},\dots,\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{N}
, each bounded as

-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\leq\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}.

(283)

Applying Hoeffding’s inequality, for any
q>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\mathbb{E}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}\right|\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{q\log N}{mN}}\right)\leq N^{-q}.

(284)

Observe that this can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\mathbb{E}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}=\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\mathbb{E}\bm{\gamma}_{n}\right):=\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\zeta}.

(285)

Therefore, by Hoeffding’s inequality (cf. (
284
)),

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\zeta}\right|\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{q\log N}{mN}}\right)\leq N^{-q}.

(286)

To bound
\left\|\bm{\zeta}\right\|_{2}
, we use the dual norm identity

\left\|\bm{\zeta}\right\|_{2}=\sup_{\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{2}=1}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\zeta}.

(287)

We apply an
\varepsilon
-cover argument to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{2}=1}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\zeta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\max_{\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{C}_{\varepsilon}}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\zeta}

\displaystyle\leq 2\max_{\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{C}_{1/2}}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\zeta}.

(288)

We have shown that for any fixed
\bm{\alpha}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\zeta}\right|\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{q\log N}{mN}}\right)\leq N^{-q}.

(289)

Therefore, for all fixed
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{C}_{1/2}
,

\left|\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\zeta}\right|\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{q\log N}{mN}}\quad\text{with probability at most }N^{-q}.

(290)

Then,

\max_{\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{C}_{1/2}}\left|\bm{\alpha}^{\top}\bm{\zeta}\right|\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{q\log N}{mN}}\quad\text{with probability at most }|\mathcal{C}_{1/2}|N^{-q}.

(291)

Recall that the covering number satisfies

|\mathcal{C}_{\varepsilon}|\leq\left(\frac{3B}{\varepsilon}\right)^{d}.

(292)

For
B=1
and
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{2}
, we have

|\mathcal{C}_{1/2}|\leq 6^{d}.

(293)

We can therefore write

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\bm{\zeta}\right\|_{2}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{q\log N}{mN}}\right)\leq 6^{d}\cdot N^{-q}.

(294)

We want this probability to be sufficiently small. Set
q=d
, so that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\bm{\zeta}\right\|_{2}\gtrsim 2\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)\leq\left(\frac{N}{6}\right)^{-d}.

(295)

Hence, the deviation is bounded with high probability:

\left\|\bm{\zeta}\right\|_{2}>\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)\quad\text{with probability at most }\mathcal{O}(N^{-d}).

(296)

Or equivalently, with probability at most
\mathcal{O}(N^{-d})
,

\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\mathbb{E}\bm{\gamma}_{n}\right\|_{2}>\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(297)

That is, with high probability
1-\mathcal{O}(N^{-d})
, we have

\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\mathbb{E}\bm{\gamma}_{n}\right\|_{2}\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(298)

Using the identities

-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bm{\gamma}_{n},\quad-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}=\mathbb{E}\bm{\gamma}_{n},

(299)

we conclude that, with high probability,

\left\|\left(-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}\right)-\left(-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}\right)\right\|_{2}=\left\|\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(300)

Using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\bm{o}_{+}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}\quad\text{(since }\|\bm{o}_{+}\|_{2}=1\text{)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(301)

Therefore, we obtain

\begin{split}\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)&\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\\
&\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).\end{split}

(302)

By pairing (
274
) with the given the conditions on
\bm{w}_{\Delta}
in (
265
), we can write

\begin{split}&\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\\
