\geq&\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{2})\right)\left[\left(1-\sigma(q_{3})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(1-\sigma(p_{3})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right]-\mathcal{O}(\tau)\end{split}

(303)

and

\begin{split}&\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\\
\leq&\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{2})\right)\left[\left(1-\sigma(p_{3})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(1-\sigma(q_{3})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\tau)\end{split}

(304)

Therefore, we can obtain the lower bound and the upper bound of
\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle
as

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{2})\right)\Big[&\left(1-\sigma(q_{3})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}\\
&-\left(1-\sigma(p_{3})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\Big]-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau)\\
\leq&\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\end{split}

(305)

and

\begin{split}&\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\\
\leq&\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{2})\right)\left[\left(1-\sigma(p_{3})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}-2}-\left(1-\sigma(q_{3})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}-2}\right]\\
&\quad+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).\end{split}

(306)

This concludes the proof of (
106
) and (
107
) in Lemma
C.1
.

To obtain
\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle
, we have to consider
\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right]
.

If
{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}\bm{o}_{-}>0
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\!\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\left[2+\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\cdot\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{j})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}-2}\right]\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(307)

If
{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}}\bm{o}_{-}\leq 0
,

\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(308)

From (
264
), We know that

\begin{split}\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle&=\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\\
&\quad-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle.\end{split}

(309)

Hence, combining both cases, we conclude

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\left[2+\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\cdot\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{j})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}-2}\right]-\mathcal{O}(\tau)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(310)

From (
300
), similar to (
302
), we can write

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle-\mathcal{O}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(311)

Hence, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\left[2+\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\cdot\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{j})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}-2}\right]-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(312)

This concludes the proof of (
108
) and (
109
) in Lemma
C.1
.

Now consider
\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\text{ for }j\neq 1,2
.

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle:=\left\langle I_{1},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle I_{2},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle.

(313)

Because
\bm{o}_{j}\text{ for }j\neq 1,2
is identical in both
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
,
\left\langle I_{1},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle I_{2},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)
. Hence,
\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)
.
From (
300
), similar to (
302
), we can write

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(314)

Therefore,

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)\text{ for }j\neq 1,2.

(315)

This concludes the proof of (
110
) in Lemma
C.1
.

∎

E.2
Proof of Lemma
C.2

Proof.

By definition, for any unlucky neuron
i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}\setminus\mathcal{W}(0)
, we have

\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{o}_{+}\leq 0.

(316)

We first consider the alignment with
\bm{o}_{+}
. That is,

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle.

(317)

The gradient is given in (
263
).
We only need to consider the cases where
\left\langle\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle>0
.
However, since
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{o}_{+}\leq 0
, we have

\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)=0.

(318)

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(319)

We know by (
302
),

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(320)

Hence,

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(321)

We now analyze the alignment with
\bm{o}_{-}
. To obtain the bound on

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle
,
we consider the expectation

\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right].

If
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}\bm{o}_{-}>0
, the inner product satisfies

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\left[2+\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\cdot\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{j})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}-2}\right]\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(322)

If
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}\bm{o}_{-}\leq 0
, then

\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(323)

From (
264
), We know that

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle.

(324)

Hence, combining both cases, we conclude

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\left[2+\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\cdot\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{j})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}-2}\right]-\mathcal{O}(\tau).

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(325)

From (
300
), similar to (
302
), we can write

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(326)

Hence,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\cdot\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\left[2+\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{-})\right)\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{+})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\cdot\left(1-\sigma({\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}}^{\top}\bm{o}_{j})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}-2}\right]-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{-}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(327)

Now consider
\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\text{ for }j\neq 1,2
.

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=+1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{z=-1}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right],\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle:=\left\langle I_{1},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle I_{2},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle.

(328)

Because
\bm{o}_{j}\text{ for }j\neq 1,2
is identical in both
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
,
\left\langle I_{1},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle I_{2},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)
. Hence,
\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau)
.
From (
300
), similar to (
302
), we can write

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(329)

Therefore,

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{j}\right\rangle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)\text{ for }j\neq 1,2.

(330)

∎

E.3
Proof of Lemma
C.3

By symmetry, the proof is analogous to that of Lemma
C.1
; Please see Appendix
E.1
.

E.4
Proof of Lemma
C.4

By symmetry, the proof is analogous to that of Lemma
C.2
; Please see Appendix
E.2
.

E.5
Proof of Lemma
C.5

Proof.

The gradient of the loss with respect to
\bm{w}_{\Delta}
for the
n^{\text{th}}
sample is given by

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{\ell}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left(\bm{W}_{B}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}\right)^{\top}\left(\bm{W}_{C}^{\top}\bm{x}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\sigma\left(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\prod_{r=s+1}^{l}\left(1-\sigma\left(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{r}^{(n)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\left(1-\sigma\left(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}\right)\right)\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}-\sum_{j=s+1}^{l}\left(1-\sigma\left(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{j}^{(n)}\right)\right)\bm{x}_{j}^{(n)}\right]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{l}\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)}.

(331)

We define the gradient summand
\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)}
as

\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)}=\beta_{s,s}\cdot\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}-\sum_{j=s+1}^{l}\beta_{s,j}\bm{x}_{j}^{(n)},

(332)

where the coefficients
\beta_{s,s}
and
\beta_{s,j}
are given by

\displaystyle\beta_{s,s}

\displaystyle=(\bm{W}_{B}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})^{\top}(\bm{W}_{C}^{\top}\bm{x}_{l}^{(n)})(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\prod_{r=s+1}^{l}\left(1-\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{r}^{(n)})\right)\right](1-\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})).

(333)

and

\displaystyle\beta_{s,j}

\displaystyle=(\bm{W}_{B}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})^{\top}(\bm{W}_{C}^{\top}\bm{x}_{l}^{(n)})(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)})

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\prod_{r=s+1}^{l}\left(1-\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{r}^{(n)})\right)\right](1-\sigma(\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{j}^{(n)})).

(334)

If we consider the gradient of the empirical loss,

\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}}=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{l}\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)}.

(335)

We are given that

p_{1}\leq\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\leq q_{1},\quad\text{and}\quad r_{1}^{*}\leq\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t+1)}}^{\top},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\leq s_{1}^{*}.

(336)

From our initialization, for all
i\in\mathcal{K}^{+}
, we have
v_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}
. This gives

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{\ell}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle.

(337)

Averaging over the training samples, the inner product of the empirical gradient becomes

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n:z^{(n)}=+1}\frac{1}{L}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right)\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n:z^{(n)}=-1}\frac{-1}{L}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right)\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\right].

(338)

First, we focus on the contribution from the samples where
z^{(n)}=+1
, for which we seek a lower bound. We analyze the inner terms by considering four cases.

Case I:

l=L_{1}^{+},\;s=L_{1}^{+}

Since
l=s
and
\bm{x}_{s}=\bm{o}_{+}
, it follows from (
332
) that

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=\beta_{s,s}.

(339)

Using (
333
), with
\bm{W}_{B}=\bm{W}_{C}=I
and
\bm{x}_{l}=\bm{x}_{s}=\bm{o}_{+}
, we obtain

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=\beta_{s,s}=\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t+1)}}^{\top},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\cdot\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle)\right).

(340)

Given the conditions in (
336
), we can write

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq\left(r_{1}^{*}-\mathcal{O}(\tau)\right)\cdot\sigma(p_{1}-\mathcal{O}(\tau))\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1}+\mathcal{O}(\tau))\right).

(341)

We can approximate
\sigma(p_{1}-\mathcal{O}(\tau))\approx\sigma(p_{1})-\mathcal{O}(\tau)
and
1-\sigma(q_{1}+\mathcal{O}(\tau))\approx 1-\sigma(q_{1})-\mathcal{O}(\tau)
, since
\mathcal{O}(\tau)<\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{d})
.

Therefore, we obtain

\begin{split}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle&\geq\left(r_{1}^{*}-\mathcal{O}(\tau)\right)\cdot\left(\sigma(p_{1})-\mathcal{O}(\tau)\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})-\mathcal{O}(\tau)\right)\\
&\geq r_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau).\end{split}

(342)

Case II:

l=L_{2}^{+},\;s=L_{2}^{+}

This configuration yields the same result as in Case I. We again obtain

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq r_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(343)

Case III:

l=L_{2}^{+},\;s=L_{1}^{+}

Comparing (
333
) with (
334
), we see that the two expressions differ only in their last term.
In this setting,
\bm{x}_{j}
equals
\bm{o}_{+}
only when
j=L_{2}^{+}
.
Consequently,
\bm{x}_{s}=\bm{x}_{j}=\bm{o}_{+}
, which implies
\beta_{s,s}=\beta_{s,j}
.
Hence,

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=\beta_{s,s}-\beta_{s,j}=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(344)

Case IV:
Others

For the other token positions,
\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=0
due to orthogonality among the features.

Combining the above, the total contribution becomes

\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq 2r_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(345)

We now bound the entire sum over all tokens:

\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot 1\cdot 2r_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(346)

Let
\rho_{t}^{+}=|\mathcal{W}(t)|
be the number of contributing neurons. Then the total contribution from the active neurons is lower bounded as

\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq\frac{2r_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\rho_{t}^{+}-\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(347)

Next, we consider
z^{(n)}=-1
for
i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}
. For
z^{(n)}=-1
, the negative sample also contains two
\bm{o}_{+}
features.

Similar to the above, we have to consider 4 cases.

Case I:

l=L_{1}^{+},\;s=L_{1}^{+}

Since
l=s
, it follows from (
332
) that

\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)}=\beta_{s,s}\cdot\bm{x}_{l}.

(348)

Since
\bm{x}_{l}=\bm{o}_{+}
, we have

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=\beta_{s,s}.

(349)

We now seek an upper bound for this contribution. From the initial conditions in (
336
), we know

\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t+1)}}^{\top},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\leq s_{1}^{*}+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(350)

Hence, we obtain

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq\left(s_{1}^{*}+\mathcal{O}(\tau)\right)\cdot\sigma(q_{1}+\mathcal{O}(\tau))\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1}-\mathcal{O}(\tau))\right).

(351)

We can approximate
\sigma(q_{1}+\mathcal{O}(\tau))\approx\sigma(q_{1})+\mathcal{O}(\tau)
and
1-\sigma(p_{1}-\mathcal{O}(\tau))\approx 1-\sigma(p_{1})+\mathcal{O}(\tau)
, since
\mathcal{O}(\tau)<\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{d})
.

Therefore, we obtain

\begin{split}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle&\leq\left(s_{1}^{*}+\mathcal{O}(\tau)\right)\cdot\left(\sigma(q_{1})+\mathcal{O}(\tau)\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})+\mathcal{O}(\tau)\right)\\
&\leq s_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).\end{split}

(352)

Case II:

l=L_{2}^{+},\;s=L_{2}^{+}

This configuration yields the same result as in Case I. We again obtain

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq s_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(353)

Case III:

l=L_{2}^{+}
,
s=L_{1}^{+}

In this case, the contribution vanishes:

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(354)

Case IV:
Others

For the other token positions,
\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=0
due to orthogonality among the features.

The maximum number of such contributing neurons is
\frac{m}{2}
. Therefore, the total contribution is bounded above by

\begin{split}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\,\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq&\frac{2s_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\frac{m}{2}+\mathcal{O}(\tau)\\
=&\sqrt{m}\cdot s_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).\end{split}

(355)

Thirdly, let us consider the contribution for
z^{(n)}=+1
from
i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}
.
From our initialization, for
i\in\mathcal{K}^{-}
,
v_{i}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}
. For
z^{(n)}=+1
, we seek an upper bound on the contribution from such neurons.

Let
z^{(n)}=+1
. To maximize the term
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{x}_{s}^{(n)}
in (
333
), we can consider the token locations which contain
\bm{o}_{-}
features since
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}
has a large component in the
\bm{o}_{-}
direction. Then
\bm{x}_{l}=\bm{o}_{-}\Rightarrow\bm{y}_{l}
contains the
\bm{o}_{-}
feature.

However, in this case,
\bm{x}_{s}=\bm{o}_{-}=\bm{x}_{l}
, and due to orthogonality,

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=0.

(356)

Hence, we only need to consider time steps
l=L_{1}^{+},L_{2}^{+}
, where
\bm{o}_{+}
features appear.

Recall that

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{\ell}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle.

(357)

We analyze the inner contributions case by case.

Case I:

l=L_{1}^{+}
,
s=L_{1}^{+}

Given that

\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{o}_{+}\leq\delta_{1}+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)=:c,

(358)

we obtain

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq c\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(359)

Case II:

l=L_{2}^{+}
,
s=L_{2}^{+}

This configuration yields the same bound:

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq c\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(360)

Case III:

l=L_{2}^{+}
,
s=L_{1}^{+}

In this case, the contribution vanishes:

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=0\pm\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(361)

Case IV:
Others

For the other token positions,
\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle=0
due to orthogonality among the features.

Thus, the total contribution from each
i\in\mathcal{K}^{-}
satisfies

\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\leq 2c\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(362)

The maximum number of such contributing neurons is
\frac{m}{2}
, so the full contribution is bounded by

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle&\leq\frac{2c\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\frac{m}{2}+\mathcal{O}(\tau)\\
&=\sqrt{m}c\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau).\end{split}

(363)

Therefore, the overall contribution is

-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq-\sqrt{m}c\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(364)

Finally, we consider
z^{(n)}=-1
for
i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}
.
For
z^{(n)}=-1
, we want a lower bound since
v_{i}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}
.

We could consider
l=L^{+}\Rightarrow\bm{x}_{l}=\bm{o}_{+}
, and write

\left\langle\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq\delta_{1}-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right).

(365)

However, the minimum number of such contributing neurons is not tractable. Thus, if we consider the worst case where
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}
for
i\in\mathcal{K}^{-}
does not learn the
\bm{o}_{+}
feature, the obvious lower bound is zero:

\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle\geq 0.

(366)

We now combine the bounds for the four terms identified in (
338
), corresponding to the contributions from:
(i)
\mathcal{K}_{+}
with
z^{(n)}=+1
as shown in (
347
),
(ii)
\mathcal{K}_{+}
with
z^{(n)}=-1
as shown in (
355
),
(iii)
\mathcal{K}_{-}
with
z^{(n)}=+1
as shown in (
364
), and
(iv)
\mathcal{K}_{-}
with
z^{(n)}=-1
as shown in (
366
). We assume the batch is balanced, so the number of positive and negative samples is equal, with each class contributing
\frac{N}{2}
samples. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{+}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{2r_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\rho_{t}^{+}-\sqrt{m}\cdot c\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\left.-\sqrt{m}\cdot s_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)+0\right]-\mathcal{O}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma(p_{1})\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)r_{1}^{*}\cdot\rho_{t}^{+}}{\sqrt{m}}-\frac{\sigma(q_{1})\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)s_{1}^{*}\cdot\sqrt{m}}{2}

(367)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)-\mathcal{O}(\tau).

(368)

where we have used the fact
\frac{\sqrt{m}}{2}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)\cdot c=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)
since
c=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{mN}}\right)
.

∎

E.6
Proof of Lemma
C.6

Proof.

The gradient is given in (
331
).

Let’s consider the alignment with
\bm{o}_{k}\text{ for }k\neq 1,2
.

\left\langle-\frac{\partial\mathcal{\ell}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle=\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle

(369)

From our initialization, for all
i\in\mathcal{K}^{+}
, we have
v_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}
.

We first consider the case
z^{(n)}=+1
for
i\in\mathcal{K}^{+}
.
Since
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}
, for
i\in\mathcal{K}^{+}
has a large
\bm{o}_{+}
component, we have to consider the token features with
\bm{o}_{+}
.
For
z^{(n)}=+1
, only when
l=L_{2}^{+},\;s=L_{1}^{+}
we have
\bm{x}_{l}=\bm{x}_{s}=\bm{o}_{+}
.
Therefore,
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{x}_{s}
is significant. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=s+1}^{l}\beta_{s,j}\langle\bm{x}_{j}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\beta_{s,s+1}\qquad\qquad(\text{Assuming W.L.O.G. }\bm{x}_{s+1}^{(n)}=\bm{o}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t+1)}}^{\top},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\cdot\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle)\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{k}\rangle)\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}.

(370)

Using the the conditions in (
336
), we can write

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\leq\left(-r_{1}^{*}+\mathcal{O}(\tau)\right)\cdot\sigma(p_{1}+\mathcal{O}(\tau))\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1}-\mathcal{O}(\tau))\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{2}-\mathcal{O}(\tau))\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}.

(371)

We can approximate
\sigma(p_{1}+\mathcal{O}(\tau))\approx\sigma(p_{1})+\mathcal{O}(\tau)
,
1-\sigma(q_{1}-\mathcal{O}(\tau))\approx 1-\sigma(q_{1})+\mathcal{O}(\tau)
and
1-\sigma(q_{2}-\mathcal{O}(\tau))\approx 1-\sigma(q_{2})+\mathcal{O}(\tau)
, since
\mathcal{O}(\tau)<\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{d})
.

Hence, we obtain

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\!\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\\
\leq&\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot 1\cdot\left[-r_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\tau)).\end{split}

(372)

Let
\rho_{t}^{+}=|\mathcal{W}(t)|
be the number of contributing neurons. Then the total contribution from
\mathcal{K}_{+}
neurons is bounded as

\begin{split}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\!\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle&\leq-\frac{r_{1}^{*}}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)\\
&\quad\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}\cdot\rho_{t}^{+}+\mathcal{O}(\tau)).\end{split}

(373)

Next, we consider
z^{(n)}=-1
for
i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}
. Since
\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle<0
, we require a lower bound for this.

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=s+1}^{l}\beta_{s,j}\langle\bm{x}_{j}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\rangle

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle-\langle{\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}^{(t+1)}}^{\top},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle\cdot\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{+}\rangle)\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(\langle\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)},\bm{o}_{k}\rangle)\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}.

(374)

Using the the conditions in (
336
), we can write

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\gtrsim-s_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(p_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}.

(375)

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\\
\gtrsim&-\frac{s_{1}^{*}}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)\left(1-\sigma(p_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\cdot\rho_{t}^{+}.\end{split}

(376)

Since
\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}\gg\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}
, this term is negligible which leads to

\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\geq-\mathcal{O}\left(\left(1-\sigma(p_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right)\approx 0.

(377)

Thirdly, we consider the case
i\in\mathcal{K}^{-}
, for
z^{(n)}=-1
. Similar to (
370
) and(
371
), when
l=L_{2}^{-},\;s=L_{1}^{-}
the contribution is significant.

\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\leq-r_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}+\mathcal{O}(\tau)).

(378)

Hence, we obtain

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\\
\leq&\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot 1\cdot\left[-r_{1}^{*}\cdot\sigma(p_{1})\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)\cdot\left(1-\sigma(q_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\tau))\end{split}

(379)

Let
\rho_{t}^{-}=|\mathcal{U}(t)|
be the number of contributing neurons. Then the total contribution from
\mathcal{K}_{-}
neurons is bounded as

\begin{split}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\,\phi^{\prime}\!\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\big\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\big\rangle&\leq-\frac{r_{1}^{*}}{\sqrt{m}}\,\sigma(p_{1})\,\bigl(1-\sigma(q_{1})\bigr)\,\bigl(1-\sigma(q_{2})\bigr)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}\,\rho_{t}^{-}\\
&\quad+\mathcal{O}(\tau)).\end{split}

(380)

Finally, we consider
i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}
for
z^{(n)}=+1
. Following the same approach as in (
374
) to (
376
), we can write

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\\
\gtrsim&-\frac{s_{1}^{*}}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)\left(1-\sigma(p_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}}\cdot\rho_{t}^{-}.\end{split}

(381)

Since
\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}\gg\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}
, this term is negligible which leads to

\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\geq-\mathcal{O}\left(\left(1-\sigma(p_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}}\right)\approx 0.

(382)

Putting it together, We know

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\frac{z^{(n)}}{L}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{L}v_{i}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n:z^{(n)}=+1}\frac{1}{L}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right)\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n:z^{(n)}=-1}\frac{-1}{L}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{+}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{K}_{-}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right)\phi^{\prime}(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}^{(n)})\sum_{s=1}^{l}\left\langle\bm{I}_{l,s}^{(n)},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle\right].

(383)

We now combine the bounds for the two terms identified in equation (
383
), corresponding to the contributions from:
(i)
\mathcal{K}_{+}
with
z^{(n)}=+1
(
373
),
(ii)
\mathcal{K}_{+}
with
z^{(n)}=-1
(
381
),
(iii)
\mathcal{K}_{-}
with
z^{(n)}=+1
(
376
), and
(iv)
\mathcal{K}_{-}
with
z^{(n)}=-1
(
380
). We assume the batch is balanced, so the number of positive and negative samples is equal, with each class contributing
\frac{N}{2}
samples. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{r_{1}^{*}}{2\sqrt{m}}\sigma(p_{1})\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)\left[\left(1-\sigma(q_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}\rho_{t}^{+}+\left(1-\sigma(q_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}\rho_{t}^{-}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{s_{1}^{*}}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot\sigma(q_{1})\left(1-\sigma(p_{1})\right)\left[\mathcal{O}\left(\left(1-\sigma(p_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}}\right)\cdot\rho_{t}^{-}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(1-\sigma(p_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right)\cdot\rho_{t}^{+}\right]

(384)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(\tau))

(385)

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{r_{1}^{*}}{2\sqrt{m}}\sigma(p_{1})\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)\left[\left(1-\sigma(q_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}\rho_{t}^{+}+\left(1-\sigma(q_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}\rho_{t}^{-}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(1-\sigma(p_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{-}}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(1-\sigma(p_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{+}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau))

(386)

From (
377
) and (
382
), we can conclude

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{\partial\hat{\mathcal{L}}}{\partial\bm{w}_{\Delta}^{(t)}},\bm{o}_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{r_{1}^{*}}{2\sqrt{m}}\sigma(p_{1})\left(1-\sigma(q_{1})\right)\left[\left(1-\sigma(q_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{+}}^{+}}\rho_{t}^{+}+\left(1-\sigma(q_{2})\right)^{\Delta L_{\bm{o}_{-}}^{-}}\rho_{t}^{-}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{O}(\tau)).

(387)

∎

Appendix F
Extension to Multi-Class Classification

Consider the classification problem with four classes, where each example is
assigned a label

\bm{z}=(z_{1},z_{2})\in\{+1,-1\}^{2}

representing four distinct classes.
Similarly to the binary setting, there exist four orthogonal discriminative
patterns. In the output layer, the scalar coefficient
v_{i}
associated with
hidden neuron
i
is replaced by a two-dimensional vector

\bm{v}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Hence, we define the model output as

\bm{F}(\bm{X})=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bm{v}_{i}\,\phi\!\left(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}(\bm{X})\right).

(388)

F_{1}(\bm{X}^{(n)})=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\bm{v}_{i})_{1}\,\phi\!\bigl(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}(\bm{X}^{(n)})\bigr),

(389)

F_{2}(\bm{X}^{(n)})=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\bm{v}_{i})_{2}\,\phi\!\bigl(\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}\bm{y}_{l}(\bm{X}^{(n)})\bigr).

(390)

The dataset can be divided into four groups as

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{(\bm{X}^{(n)},\bm{z}^{(n)})\mid\bm{z}^{(n)}=(1,1)\},

(391)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2}

\displaystyle=\{(\bm{X}^{(n)},\bm{z}^{(n)})\mid\bm{z}^{(n)}=(1,-1)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{3}

\displaystyle=\{(\bm{X}^{(n)},\bm{z}^{(n)})\mid\bm{z}^{(n)}=(-1,1)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{4}

\displaystyle=\{(\bm{X}^{(n)},\bm{z}^{(n)})\mid\bm{z}^{(n)}=(-1,-1)\}.

The loss function for data
(\bm{X}^{(n)},\bm{z}^{(n)})
is

\mathrm{Loss}(\bm{X}^{(n)},\bm{z}^{(n)})=\max\left\{1-\bm{z}^{(n)\top}\bm{F}(\bm{X}^{(n)}),\,0\right\}.

(392)

Since
\bm{v}_{i}\in\{\pm\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\}^{2}
, we divide neurons into
four groups:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{1}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{i:\bm{v}_{i}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{m}}(1,1)\bigr\},

(393)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{i:\bm{v}_{i}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{m}}(1,-1)\bigr\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{3}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{i:\bm{v}_{i}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{m}}(-1,1)\bigr\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{4}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{i:\bm{v}_{i}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{m}}(-1,-1)\bigr\}.

For neuron
i
, the gradient decomposes as

\frac{\partial\mathrm{Loss}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}}=-z_{1}^{(n)}\,\frac{\partial F_{1}(\bm{X}^{(n)})}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}}-z_{2}^{(n)}\,\frac{\partial F_{2}(\bm{X}^{(n)})}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}}.

(394)

Let
\bm{o}_{1},\bm{o}_{2},\bm{o}_{3},\bm{o}_{4}
denote the four discriminative
directions.
Consider
i\in\mathcal{W}_{2}
, i.e.

\bm{v}_{i}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{m}}(1,-1)
.
Projecting the gradient onto
\bm{o}_{2}
, for any

(\bm{X}^{(n)},\bm{z}^{(n)})\in\mathcal{D}_{2}
we obtain

-\Bigl\langle\frac{\partial\mathrm{Loss}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}},\bm{o}_{2}\Bigr\rangle\approx\frac{2}{\sqrt{m}}\|\bm{o}_{2}\|^{2}\;>\;0,

(395)

showing GD moves
\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}
toward
\bm{o}_{2}
.

For samples from the other classes:

\displaystyle(\bm{X}^{(n)},\bm{z}^{(n)})\in\mathcal{D}_{1}:

\displaystyle-\left\langle\frac{\partial\mathrm{Loss}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}},\bm{o}_{1}\right\rangle\approx 0,

(396)

\displaystyle(\bm{X}^{(n)},\bm{z}^{(n)})\in\mathcal{D}_{3}:

\displaystyle-\left\langle\frac{\partial\mathrm{Loss}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}},\bm{o}_{3}\right\rangle\approx-\frac{2}{\sqrt{m}}\|\bm{o}_{3}\|^{2},

\displaystyle(\bm{X}^{(n)},\bm{z}^{(n)})\in\mathcal{D}_{4}:

\displaystyle-\left\langle\frac{\partial\mathrm{Loss}}{\partial\bm{W}_{O(i,\cdot)}},\bm{o}_{4}\right\rangle\approx 0.

Thus, for
i\in\mathcal{W}_{2}
, the update direction aligns with
\bm{o}_{2}
,
and similarly neurons in
\mathcal{W}_{1},\mathcal{W}_{3},\mathcal{W}_{4}
align with

\bm{o}_{1},\bm{o}_{3},\bm{o}_{4}
respectively.
Similarly, we can analyze the gradient dynamics of the gating vector
\bm{w}_{\Delta}
.
